<div class='question question'>

 A man travels $200 \ \text{miles}$ at an average speed of $60 \ \text{miles per hour}$.
 How long does the journey take in hours and minutes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Time taken}  &= \dfrac {200} {60} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {20 \times 10} {20 \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 10} {\cancel {20} \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10} {3} \\\\
                    &= 3 \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                    &= 3 \ \text {hours} + 60 \times \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                    &= 3 \ \text {hours} \ 20 \ \text {minutes}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \ \text {hours} \ 20 \ \text {minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Time taken}  &= \dfrac {200} {60} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {20 \times 10} {20 \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 10} {\cancel {20} \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10} {3} \\\\
                    &= 3 \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                    &= 3 \ \text {hours} + 60 \times \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                    &= 3 \ \text {hours} \ 20 \ \text {minutes}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Two runners are having a race. Gary starts running from the start line at $10 \ \text{m/s}$. 
Two seconds later Andy starts running from the start line at $12 \ \text{m/s}$.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long after Gary starts running does Andy catch up with him?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


Let the time taken by Gary to catch up (in seconds) $= t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \times (t - 2)  &=  10 \times t \\\\
12t - 24           &=  10t \\\\
12t - 10t          &=  24 \\\\
2t                 &=  24 \\\\
t                  &=  \dfrac{24}{2} \\\\
t                  &=  \dfrac{12 \times 2}{2} \\\\
t                  &=  \dfrac{12 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
t                  &=  12 \text{ seconds}
\end{aligned}
$

Time taken $= 12 \text{ seconds}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \text{ seconds}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

t                  &=  \dfrac{24}{2} \\\\
t                  &=  \dfrac{12 \times 2}{2} \\\\
t                  &=  \dfrac{12 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
t                  &=  12 \text{ seconds}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Time taken $= 12 \text{ seconds}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

A train leaves London and travels at a steady speed of $120 \ \text{mph}$ to Edinburgh. 
Before reaching Edinburgh, the train stops in Leeds after travelling for one and a half hours. 
The distance from London to Edinburgh is $420 \ \text{miles}$. Use this information to answer the following:

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long does the train take to travel from London to Edinburgh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}  &=  \dfrac{420 \text{ miles}}{120 \text{ mph}} \\\\
             &=  \dfrac{420}{120} \\\\
             &=  \dfrac{7 \times 6 \times 10}{2 \times 6 \times 10} \\\\
             &=  \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{6} \times \cancel{10}}{2 \times \cancel{6} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
             &=  \dfrac{7}{2} \\\\
             &=  3.5 \text{ hours or } 3 \text{ hours and } 30 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3.5 \text{ hours or } 3 \text{ hours and } 30 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}  &=  \dfrac{420 \text{ miles}}{120 \text{ mph}} \\\\
             &=  \dfrac{420}{120} \\\\
             &=  \dfrac{7 \times 6 \times 10}{2 \times 6 \times 10} \\\\
             &=  \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{6} \times \cancel{10}}{2 \times \cancel{6} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
             &=  \dfrac{7}{2} \\\\
             &=  3.5 \text{ hours or } 3 \text{ hours and } 30 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3.5 \text{ hours or } 3 \text{ hours and } 30 \text{ minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How far is Leeds from London?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance}  &=  120 \times (1 \text{ hour } 30 \text{ minutes}) \\\\
                 &=  120 \times 1.5 \\\\
                 &=  180 \text{ miles}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$180 \text{ miles}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance}  &=  120 \times (1 \text{ hour } 30 \text{ minutes}) \\\\
                 &=  120 \times 1.5 \\\\
                 &=  180 \text{ miles}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Rio travels directly from $A$ to $C$ at a speed of $80 \ \text{km/hr}$.

Harry travels from $A$ to $B$ to $C$ at $100 \ \text{km/hr}$.

Fahad travels directly from $A$ to $C$ at $60 \ \text{km/hr}$.

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-16-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-16-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-16-00.png")

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long (in hours and minutes) does each person take?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by Rio}   &=  \dfrac{200}{80} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{4 \times 5 \times 10}{4 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{\cancel{4} \times 5 \times \cancel{10}}{\cancel{4} \times 2 \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{5}{2} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  2\dfrac{1}{2} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  2 \text{ hours } 30 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Harry} &=  \dfrac{150 + 150}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{300}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{3 \times 100}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{100}} \\\\
                           &=  3 \text{ hours} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Fahad} &=  \dfrac{200}{60} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 10}{3 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times \cancel{2} \times 5 \times \cancel{10}}{3 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times 5}{3} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{10}{3} \\\\
                           &=  3\dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  3 \text{ hours } 20 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Rio takes $2 \text{ hours } 30 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Harry takes $3 \text{ hours}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Fahad takes $3 \text{ hours } 20 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by Rio}   &=  \dfrac{200}{80} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{4 \times 5 \times 10}{4 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{\cancel{4} \times 5 \times \cancel{10}}{\cancel{4} \times 2 \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{5}{2} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  2\dfrac{1}{2} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  2 \text{ hours } 30 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Harry} &=  \dfrac{150 + 150}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{300}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{3 \times 100}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{100}} \\\\
                           &=  3 \text{ hours} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Fahad} &=  \dfrac{200}{60} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 10}{3 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times \cancel{2} \times 5 \times \cancel{10}}{3 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times 5}{3} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{10}{3} \\\\
                           &=  3\dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  3 \text{ hours } 20 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

He leaves home and cycles for $10 \ \text{km}$ at a speed of $30 \ \text{km per hour}$ . 

How many minutes does this take him?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}   &= \dfrac{10}{30} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{10}{10 \times 3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{\cancel{10}}{\cancel{10} \times 3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours} \\\\
              &= 60 \times \dfrac{1}{3} \text{ minutes} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{60}{3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{20 \times 3}{3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{20 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
              &= 20 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time

              &= \dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours} \\\\
              &= 60 \times \dfrac{1}{3} \text{ minutes} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{60}{3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{20 \times 3}{3} \\\\

$

              &= 60 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \text{ minutes} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{60}{4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 4}{4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
              &= 15 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

                      &=  10{:}35
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='papername'>
<p>benenden-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1:2:1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>

tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A mouse is trying to climb out of a hole which is $220 \ \text{cm}$ deep.
Each hour, she manages to climb $40 \ \text{cm}$, but slips back $10 \ \text{cm}$ when she stops for a rest. 
How many hours will it take her to reach the top of the hole?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}   &=  40 - 10 \\\\
               &=  30 \text{ cm per hour} \\\\
\text{Time}    &=  \dfrac{220}{30} \\\\
               &=  \dfrac{22\cancel{0}}{3\cancel{0}} \\\\
               &=  \dfrac{22}{3} \\\\
               &=  7\dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7\dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}   &=  40 - 10 \\\\
               &=  30 \text{ cm per hour} \\\\
\text{Time}    &=  \dfrac{220}{30} \\\\
               &=  \dfrac{22\cancel{0}}{3\cancel{0}} \\\\
               &=  \dfrac{22}{3} \\\\
               &=  7\dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7\dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>06304292-3FB2-45D8-B196-9139212A69A7</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>benenden-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1:2:2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>

tspeed
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

A train is $1 \ \text{km}$ long and travels through a tunnel which is $1.5 \ \text{km}$ long. 
If it travels at a constant speed of $60 \ \text{km/hr}$, how long will it take the whole train to pass through the tunnel?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}     &= \dfrac{1 + 1.5}{60} \\\\
                &= \dfrac{2.5}{60} \text{ hours} \\\\
                &= 60 \times \dfrac{2.5}{60} \text{ minutes} \\\\
                &= \dfrac{2.5 \times 60}{60} \\\\
                &= \dfrac{2.5 \times \cancel{60}}{\cancel{60}} \\\\
                &= 2.5 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2.5 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}     &= \dfrac{1 + 1.5}{60} \\\\
                &= \dfrac{2.5}{60} \text{ hours} \\\\
                &= 60 \times \dfrac{2.5}{60} \text{ minutes} \\\\
                &= \dfrac{2.5 \times 60}{60} \\\\
                &= \dfrac{2.5 \times \cancel{60}}{\cancel{60}} \\\\
                &= 2.5 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2.5 \text{ minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>city-of-london-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-group-2:1:17</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

I travel $1\ \text{mile}$ at $60\ \text{m.p.h.}$ and then $1\ \text{mile}$ at $30\ \text{m.p.h.}$ What is my average speed in miles per hour?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Total distance}} {\text{Total time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total distance}  &= 1 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 2 \ \text{miles} \\\\
\text {Time 1}          &= \dfrac {1} {60} \ \text{hour} \\\\
\text {Time 2}          &= \dfrac {1} {30} \ \text{hour} \\\\
\text {Total time}      &= \dfrac {1} {60} + \dfrac {1} {30} \\\\
\text {Average speed}   &= \dfrac {2} {\dfrac {1} {60} + \dfrac {1} {30}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {\dfrac {1} {60} + \dfrac {2} {30 \times 2}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {\dfrac {1} {60} + \dfrac {2} {60}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {\dfrac {3} {60}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 60} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 20 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 20 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
                        &= 40 \ \text {m.p.h}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40 \ \text {m.p.h}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_average_speed
<emptyline>

                        &= 2 \ \text{miles} \\\\

\text {Time 2}          &= \dfrac {1} {30} \ \text{hour} \\\\
\text {Total time}      &= \dfrac {1} {60} + \dfrac {1} {30} \\\\
\text {Average speed}   &= \dfrac {2} {\dfrac {1} {60} + \dfrac {1} {30}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {\dfrac {1} {60} + \dfrac {2} {30 \times 2}} \\\\

$

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prpcc question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>5612B8B3-1CD7-4B9F-A4AF-FC459A544D71</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>city-of-london-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1:1:10</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prpcc</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a sponsored walk: Adam took $4\ \text{hours}$, $39\ \text{minutes}$; 

Billy took $274\ \text{minutes}$ and Charlie took $4\dfrac{3}{5}\ \text{hours}$.

Who was the quickest and who was the slowest?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by Adam}   &= 4 \text { hours } 39 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                            &= 4 \times 60 + 39 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                            &= 4 \times 60 + 39 \\\\
                            &= 240 + 39 \\\\
                            &= 279 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Billy}  &= 274 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Charlie}&= 4\dfrac{3}{5} \text { hours} \\\\
                            &= 4 \times 60 + \dfrac{3}{5} \times 60 \text { minutes} \\\\
                            &= 240 + \dfrac{3 \times 60}{5} \\\\
                            &= 240 + \dfrac{3 \times 12 \times 5}{5} \\\\
                            &= 240 + \dfrac{3 \times 12 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
                            &= 240 + 3 \times 12 \\\\
                            &= 240 + 36 \\\\
                            &= 276 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

Quickest = Billy

Slowest = Adam

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Billy and Adam

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by Adam}   &= 4 \text { hours } 39 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                            &= 4 \times 60 + 39 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                            &= 4 \times 60 + 39 \\\\
                            &= 240 + 39 \\\\
                            &= 279 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Billy}  &= 274 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Charlie}&= 4\dfrac{3}{5} \text { hours} \\\\
                            &= 4 \times 60 + \dfrac{3}{5} \times 60 \text { minutes} \\\\
                            &= 240 + \dfrac{3 \times 60}{5} \\\\
                            &= 240 + \dfrac{3 \times 12 \times 5}{5} \\\\
                            &= 240 + \dfrac{3 \times 12 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
                            &= 240 + 3 \times 12 \\\\
                            &= 240 + 36 \\\\
                            &= 276 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Quickest = Billy
<emptyline>
Slowest = Adam
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Billy and Adam
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows three different routes from Peter's factory (marked as $F$) to his warehouse (marked as $W$). 
Each section of the route is either motorway (labelled $M$), main road (labelled $A$) or minor road (labelled $B$).
The length of each section of the route is also stated.

![csse--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-10-00.png](/assets/csse--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-10-00.png "csse--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-10-00.png")

Peter expects to travel at $90 \ \text{km/h}$ on the motorway, $60 \ \text{km/h}$ on the main road and $45 \ \text{km/h}$ 
on the minor road.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the total distance frrom factory ($F$) to warehouse ($W$) following route $II$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total distance}    &=   15 + 60 + 15 + 30 \\\\
                         &=   75 + 45 \\\\
                         &=   120 \text{ km}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \text{ km}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total distance}    &=   15 + 60 + 15 + 30 \\\\
                         &=   75 + 45 \\\\
                         &=   120 \text{ km}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$120 \text{ km}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the total distance of the *longest* route from factory ($F$) to warehouse ($W$)?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Longest route is route $III$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total distance}    &=   15 + 135 + 30 \\\\
                         &=   150 + 30 \\\\
                         &=   180 \text{ km}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$180 \text{ km}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Longest route is route $III$

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long, in minutes, will it take Peter to travel the first $15 \ \text{km}$ on the main road from the factory ($F$)?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}   &= \dfrac{15}{60} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15}{15 \times 4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{\cancel{15}}{\cancel{15} \times 4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{1}{4} \text{ hours} \\\\
              &= 60 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \text{ minutes} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{60}{4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 4}{4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
              &= 15 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}   &= \dfrac{15}{60} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15}{15 \times 4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{\cancel{15}}{\cancel{15} \times 4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{1}{4} \text{ hours} \\\\
              &= 60 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \text{ minutes} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{60}{4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 4}{4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
              &= 15 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long, in minutes, will it take Peter to travel $45 \ \text{km}$ on the motorway (on route $I$)?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}   &= \dfrac{45}{90} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{45}{45 \times 2} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{\cancel{45}}{\cancel{45} \times 2} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{1}{2} \text{ hours} \\\\
              &= 60 \times \dfrac{1}{2} \text{ minutes} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{60}{2} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{30 \times 2}{2} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{30 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
              &= 30 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long, in hours and minutes, will it take Peter to complete the whole of route $II$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken to cross main road}   &= \dfrac{15 + 60 + 30}{60} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{105}{60} \text{ hours} \\\\
                                       &= 60 \times \dfrac{105}{60} \text{ minutes} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{60 \times 105}{60} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{\cancel{60} \times 105}{\cancel{60}} \\\\
                                       &= 105 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Time taken to cross minor road}  &= \dfrac{15}{45} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{15}{15 \times 3} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{\cancel{15}}{\cancel{15} \times 3} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours} \\\\
                                       &= 60 \times \dfrac{1}{3} \text{ minutes} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{60}{3} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{20 \times 3}{3} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{20 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
                                       &= 20 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Total time taken}                &= 105 + 20 \\\\
                                       &= 125 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{125}{60} \text { hours} \\\\
                                       &= 2\dfrac{5}{60} \text { hours} \\\\
                                       &= 2 \text { hours } 5 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 \text { hours } 5 \text{ minutes}$

</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>csse--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-4:1:10</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>

tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A Formula $1$ race is $96$ laps of the track. A driver averages $1 \ \text{minute} \ 20 \ \text{seconds}$ per lap. How long is his race in hours and minutes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Race duration}   &= 96 \times (1 \text{ minute } 20 \text{ seconds} ) \\\\
                        &= 96 \times (60 \text{ seconds} + 20 \text{ seconds} ) \\\\
                        &= 96 \times 80 \text{ seconds} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {96 \times 80} {60} \text{ minutes} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {12 \times 8 \times 16 \times 5} {12 \times 5}  \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel{12} \times 8 \times 16 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{12} \times \cancel{5}} \\\\
                        &= 128 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                        &= 2 \ \text {hours} \ 8 \ \text {minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 \ \text {hour} \ 8 \ \text {minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Race duration}   &= 96 \times (1 \text{ minute } 20 \text{ seconds} ) \\\\
                        &= 96 \times (60 \text{ seconds} + 20 \text{ seconds} ) \\\\
                        &= 96 \times 80 \text{ seconds} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {96 \times 80} {60} \text{ minutes} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {12 \times 8 \times 16 \times 5} {12 \times 5}  \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel{12} \times 8 \times 16 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{12} \times \cancel{5}} \\\\
                        &= 128 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                        &= 2 \ \text {hours} \ 8 \ \text {minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2 \ \text {hour} \ 8 \ \text {minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

tspeed
</li>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

Rhys and Sarah are on their bicycles and start $90 \ \text{miles}$ apart on a road, riding towards each other. 
Rhys is travelling at $10 \ \text{mph}$ and Sarah is travelling at $20 \ \text{mph}$. They set off at the same time and both keep moving at constant speeds until they meet.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out at what distance each rider has cycled by the time they meet.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Rhys and Sarah meet at time (in hours) $= t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
10 \times t + 20 \times t &= 90 \\\\
10t + 20t                 &= 90 \\\\
30t                       &= 90 \\\\
t                         &= \dfrac{90}{30} \\\\
t                         &= \dfrac{3 \times 30}{30} \\\\
t                         &= \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{30}}{\cancel{30}} \\\\
t                         &= 3 \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance travelled by Rhys}  &= 10 \times t \\\\
                                   &= 10 \times 3 \\\\
                                   &= 30 \text { miles} \\\\
\text{Distance travelled by Sarah} &= 20 \times t \\\\
                                   &= 20 \times 3 \\\\
                                   &= 60 \text { miles}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \text { miles}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \text { miles}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let Rhys and Sarah meet at time (in hours) $= t$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
10 \times t + 20 \times t &= 90 \\\\
10t + 20t                 &= 90 \\\\
30t                       &= 90 \\\\
t                         &= \dfrac{90}{30} \\\\
t                         &= \dfrac{3 \times 30}{30} \\\\
t                         &= \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{30}}{\cancel{30}} \\\\
t                         &= 3 \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance travelled by Rhys}  &= 10 \times t \\\\
                                   &= 10 \times 3 \\\\
                                   &= 30 \text { miles} \\\\
\text{Distance travelled by Sarah} &= 20 \times t \\\\
                                   &= 20 \times 3 \\\\
                                   &= 60 \text { miles}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$30 \text { miles}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If a tourist cycles at $14 \ \text{kmph}$, work out how long her journey would take from Josham to Moreton in hours and minutes.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Distance}    &= \text {Josham to Langham} + \text {Langham to Moreton} \\\\
                    &= 26 + 9 \\\\
                    &= 35 \ \text {km} \\\\
\text {Time}        &= \dfrac {35} {14} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {7 \times 5} {7 \times 2} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{7} \times 5} {\cancel{7} \times 2} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {5} {2} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                    &= 2 \ \text {hours} \ 30 \ \text {minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 \ \text {hours} \ 30 \ \text {minutes}$

</div>

                    &= \dfrac {7 \times 5} {7 \times 2} \\\\

                    &= \dfrac {5} {2} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                    &= 2 \ \text {hours} \ 30 \ \text {minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate out how long the journey takes in hours and minutes.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Time taken}  &= 10 {:} 17 \ \text {pm} - 5 {:} 44 \ \text {am} \\\\
                    &= 7 \ \text{hours} \ 27 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7 \ \text{hours} \ 27 \ \text{minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Time taken}  &= 10 {:} 17 \ \text {pm} - 5 {:} 44 \ \text {am} \\\\
                    &= 7 \ \text{hours} \ 27 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

An aeroplane takes $7 \ \text{hours}$ to travel from London to New York at a
constant speed. The distance from London to New York is approximately $5600 \ \text{kilometres}$. 
Calculate the approximate speed of the aeroplane in kilometres per hour.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Speed}   &= \dfrac {5600} {7} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {800 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {800 \times \cancel {7} } {\cancel {7} } \\\\
                &= 800 \ \text {kilometres per hour}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$800 \ \text{kilometres per hour}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Speed}   &= \dfrac {5600} {7} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {800 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {800 \times \cancel {7} } {\cancel {7} } \\\\
                &= 800 \ \text {kilometres per hour}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$800 \ \text{kilometres per hour}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>dulwich-college--11-plus--maths--9999--specimen-paper-f:1:22</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

James cycles $4000 \ \text{m}$ at $10 \ \text{metres per second}$. Tom takes $100 \ \text{seconds}$ more to cycle the same distance. 
At what speed does Tom cycle? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by James}      &= \dfrac{4000}{10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{400 \cancel{0}}{1 \cancel{0}} \\\\
                                &= 400 \text{ seconds} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Tom}        &= 100 + 400 \\\\
                                &= 500 \text{ seconds} \\\\
\text{Speed for Tom}            &= \dfrac{4000}{500} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{5 \times 8 \times 100}{5 \times 100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{\cancel{5} \times 8 \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{5} \times \cancel{100}} \\\\
                                &= 8 \text{ metres per second}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \text{ metres per second}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by James}      &= \dfrac{4000}{10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{400 \cancel{0}}{1 \cancel{0}} \\\\
                                &= 400 \text{ seconds} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Tom}        &= 100 + 400 \\\\
                                &= 500 \text{ seconds} \\\\
\text{Speed for Tom}            &= \dfrac{4000}{500} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{5 \times 8 \times 100}{5 \times 100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{\cancel{5} \times 8 \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{5} \times \cancel{100}} \\\\
                                &= 8 \text{ metres per second}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \text{ metres per second}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

Calum and his friend cycle from $A$ to $C$, passing through $B$.

During the trip he asks his friend how far they have cycled.

His friend replies "one third as far as it is from here to $B$".

Ten miles later Calum asks him how far they still have to cycle to reach $C$.

His friend replies again "one third as far as it is from here to $B$".

How far from $A$ will Calum have cycled when he reaches $C?$

<div class='question question'>

What is $6 \ \text{metres per second}$ in kilometres per hour? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6 \ \text{metres per second} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6 \ \text{metres}} {1 \ \text{second}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6 } {1000} \ \text{kilometres} \times \dfrac {60 \times 60} {1 \ \text{hour}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6 \times 60 \times 60} {1000} \ \text{kilometres per hour} \\\\
&= \dfrac {21600} {1000} \ \text{kilometres per hour} \\\\
&= 21.6 \ \text{kilometres per hour}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21.6 \ \text{kilometres per hour}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6 \ \text{metres per second} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6 \ \text{metres}} {1 \ \text{second}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6 } {1000} \ \text{kilometres} \times \dfrac {60 \times 60} {1 \ \text{hour}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6 \times 60 \times 60} {1000} \ \text{kilometres per hour} \\\\
&= \dfrac {21600} {1000} \ \text{kilometres per hour} \\\\
&= 21.6 \ \text{kilometres per hour}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$21.6 \ \text{kilometres per hour}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Kasey takes $24 \ \text{minutes}$ to cycle from $A$ to $B$.

Shayban cycles at three quarters of Kasey's speed.

How long does it take Shayban to cycle from $A$ to $B?$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


Let the distance $= D$

Speed $= \dfrac{D}{24}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed of Shyaban}      &= \dfrac{1}{3} \text{ of } \dfrac{D}{24} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac{1}{3} \times \dfrac{D}{24} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac{D}{24 \times 3} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac{D}{72} \\\\
\text{Time}                  &= \dfrac{D}{\dfrac{D}{72}} \\\\
                             &= 72 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
Let the distance $= D$
<emptyline>
Speed $= \dfrac{D}{24}$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed of Shyaban}      &= \dfrac{1}{3} \text{ of } \dfrac{D}{24} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac{1}{3} \times \dfrac{D}{24} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac{D}{24 \times 3} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac{D}{72} \\\\
\text{Time}                  &= \dfrac{D}{\dfrac{D}{72}} \\\\
                             &= 72 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$72 \text{ minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

tspeed

<div class='question question'>

James is driving  on the M25. He is annoyed because a $35  \ \text{mile}$ stretch of road works has a speed restriction of $50 \ \text{mph}$ rather being able to travel at $70 \ \text{mph}$.

How much longer is his journey as a result of the roadworks?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


Let the time taken (without restriction) $= t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
t                   &= \dfrac{35}{70} \\\\
t                   &= \dfrac{35}{35 \times 2} \\\\
t                   &= \dfrac{\cancel{35}}{\cancel{35} \times 2} \\\\
t                   &= \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
t                   &= 0.5 \text{ hour} \\\\
\text{Time}         &= \dfrac{35}{50} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac{\cancel{5} \times 7}{\cancel{5} \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac{7}{10} \\\\
                    &= 0.7 \text{ hour} \\\\
\text{Extra time}   &= 0.7 - t \\\\
                    &= 0.7 - 0.5 \\\\
                    &= 0.2 \text{ hour}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.2 \text{ hour}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
Let the time taken (without restriction) $= t$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
t                   &= \dfrac{35}{70} \\\\
t                   &= \dfrac{35}{35 \times 2} \\\\
t                   &= \dfrac{\cancel{35}}{\cancel{35} \times 2} \\\\
t                   &= \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
t                   &= 0.5 \text{ hour} \\\\
\text{Time}         &= \dfrac{35}{50} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac{\cancel{5} \times 7}{\cancel{5} \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac{7}{10} \\\\
                    &= 0.7 \text{ hour} \\\\
\text{Extra time}   &= 0.7 - t \\\\
                    &= 0.7 - 0.5 \\\\
                    &= 0.2 \text{ hour}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$0.2 \text{ hour}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prpcc question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>216E7251-C33B-4F2F-95B1-0CF66751A259</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>forest-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2:1:32</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prpcc</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Issac cycles for $20 \ \text{minutes}$ at $18  \ \text{km/hr}$ and for $10 \ \text{minutes}$ at $6 \ \text{km/hr}$.
How far has he cycled and what is his average speed? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance covered}  &= 20 \text{ minute} \times 18 \text{ km/hr} + 10 \text { minute} \times 6 \text{ km/hr} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{20}{60} \text{ hour} \times 18 \text{ km/hr} + \dfrac{10}{60} \text { hour} \times 6 \text{ km/hr} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{20}{60} \times 18 + \dfrac{10}{60} \times 6 \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{20 \times 18}{60} + \dfrac{10 \times 6}{60} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{20 \times 6 \times 3}{20 \times 3} + \dfrac{10 \times 6}{10 \times 6} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{\cancel{20} \times 6 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{20} \times \cancel{3}} + \dfrac{\cancel{10} \times \cancel{6}}{\cancel{10} \times \cancel{6}} \\\\
                         &= 6 + 1 \\\\
                         &= 7 \text{ km} \\\\
\text{Average speed}     &= \dfrac{7 \text{ km}}{20 + 10 \text{ minutes}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \text{ km}}{30 \text{ minutes}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \times 60 \text{ km}}{30 \text{ hours}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \times 60}{30} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \times 30 \times 2}{30} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{30} \times 2}{\cancel{30}} \\\\
                         &= 7 \times 2 \\\\
                         &= 14 \text{ km/hr}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7 \text{ km}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$14 \text{ km/hr}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance covered}  &= 20 \text{ minute} \times 18 \text{ km/hr} + 10 \text { minute} \times 6 \text{ km/hr} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{20}{60} \text{ hour} \times 18 \text{ km/hr} + \dfrac{10}{60} \text { hour} \times 6 \text{ km/hr} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{20}{60} \times 18 + \dfrac{10}{60} \times 6 \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{20 \times 18}{60} + \dfrac{10 \times 6}{60} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{20 \times 6 \times 3}{20 \times 3} + \dfrac{10 \times 6}{10 \times 6} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{\cancel{20} \times 6 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{20} \times \cancel{3}} + \dfrac{\cancel{10} \times \cancel{6}}{\cancel{10} \times \cancel{6}} \\\\
                         &= 6 + 1 \\\\
                         &= 7 \text{ km} \\\\
\text{Average speed}     &= \dfrac{7 \text{ km}}{20 + 10 \text{ minutes}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \text{ km}}{30 \text{ minutes}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \times 60 \text{ km}}{30 \text{ hours}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \times 60}{30} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \times 30 \times 2}{30} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{30} \times 2}{\cancel{30}} \\\\
                         &= 7 \times 2 \\\\
                         &= 14 \text{ km/hr}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Chrystine's candle is $42 \ \text{cm}$ tall and burns at a rate of $3 \ \text{cm}$ every $30 \ \text{minutes}$.
Kieran's candle is $30 \ \text{cm}$ tall and burns at a rate of $2 \ \text{cm}$ every $40 \ \text{minutes}$.

They both light their candles at the same time.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much longer does Kieran's candle take to burn completely down than Chrystine's candle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Burn rate for Chrystine}   &= \dfrac{3}{30} \text { cm per minute} \\\\
\text{Time taken }               &= \dfrac {42} { \dfrac{3}{30} } \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{42 \times 30}{3} \\\\ 
                                 &= \dfrac{42 \times 3 \times 10}{3} \\\\ 
                                 &= \dfrac{42 \times \cancel{3} \times 10}{\cancel{3}} \\\\ 
                                 &= 42 \times 10 \\\\ 
                                 &= 420 \text{ minutes} \\\\ 
\text{Burn rate for Kieran}      &= \dfrac{2}{40} \text { cm per minute} \\\\
\text{Time taken }               &= \dfrac {30} { \dfrac{2}{40} } \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{30 \times 40}{2} \\\\ 
                                 &= \dfrac{30 \times 20 \times 2}{2} \\\\ 
                                 &= \dfrac{30 \times 20 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\ 
                                 &= 30 \times 20 \\\\ 
                                 &= 600 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Extra time}                &= 600 - 420 \\\\
                                 &= 180 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{180}{60} \text{ hours} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{3 \times 60}{60} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{60}}{\cancel{60}} \\\\
                                 &= 3 \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$

Kieran's candle takes $3$ hours longer to burn completely down than Chrystine's candle.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \text{ hours}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>

\begin{aligned}

                                 &= \dfrac{42 \times 30}{3} \\\\ 
                                 &= \dfrac{42 \times 3 \times 10}{3} \\\\ 
                                 &= \dfrac{42 \times \cancel{3} \times 10}{\cancel{3}} \\\\ 
                                 &= 42 \times 10 \\\\ 
                                 &= 420 \text{ minutes} \\\\ 
\text{Burn rate for Kieran}      &= \dfrac{2}{40} \text { cm per minute} \\\\
\text{Time taken }               &= \dfrac {30} { \dfrac{2}{40} } \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{30 \times 40}{2} \\\\ 
                                 &= \dfrac{30 \times 20 \times 2}{2} \\\\ 
                                 &= \dfrac{30 \times 20 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\ 
                                 &= 30 \times 20 \\\\ 
                                 &= 600 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Extra time}                &= 600 - 420 \\\\
                                 &= 180 \text{ minutes} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{180}{60} \text{ hours} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{3 \times 60}{60} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{60}}{\cancel{60}} \\\\
                                 &= 3 \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Kieran's candle takes $3$ hours longer to burn completely down than Chrystine's candle.
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

After how many minutes were the candles the same height?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


Let both candles are the same height at time (in minutes) $= t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
42 - \dfrac{3}{30} \times t                                          &= 30 - \dfrac{2}{40} \times t \\\\
42 - \dfrac{3t}{30}                                                  &= 30 - \dfrac{2t}{40} \\\\
42 - \dfrac{t}{10}                                                   &= 30 - \dfrac{t}{20} \\\\
30 - \dfrac{t}{20}                                                   &= 42 - \dfrac{t}{10} \\\\
\dfrac{t}{10} - \dfrac{t}{20}                                        &= 42 - 30 \\\\
\dfrac{2 \times t}{2 \times 10} - \dfrac{t}{20}                      &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac{2t}{20} - \dfrac{t}{20}                                       &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac{2t - t}{20}                                                   &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac{t}{20}                                                        &= 12 \\\\
t                                                                    &= 12 \times 20 \\\\
t                                                                    &= 240 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

Time $= 240 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$240 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>

\dfrac{t}{10} - \dfrac{t}{20}                                        &= 42 - 30 \\\\
\dfrac{2 \times t}{2 \times 10} - \dfrac{t}{20}                      &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac{2t}{20} - \dfrac{t}{20}                                       &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac{2t - t}{20}                                                   &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac{t}{20}                                                        &= 12 \\\\
t                                                                    &= 12 \times 20 \\\\
t                                                                    &= 240 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Time $= 240 \text{ minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>john-lyon-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:12</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>

<li>
tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A cyclist cycles $45 \ \text{kilometres}$ in $3 \ \text{hours}$. 
How many minutes does it take him to cycle $1500 \ \text{metres}$ at the same rate? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}  &= \dfrac{45}{3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 3}{3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
              &= 15 \text{ kilometres/hour} \\\\
              &= 15 \times \dfrac{1000}{60} \text{ metres/minute} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 1000}{60} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 250 \times 4}{15 \times 4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{\cancel{15} \times 250 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{15} \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
              &= 250 \text{ metres/minute}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}  &=  \dfrac{1500}{250} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{150\cancel{0}}{25\cancel{0}} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{150}{25} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{5 \times 5 \times 6}{5 \times 5} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{\cancel{5} \times \cancel{5} \times 6}{\cancel{5} \times \cancel{5}} \\\\
                   &=  6 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternative solution

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


Let the time taken in minutes $= t$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{1500}{t}  &= \dfrac{45 \text{ kilometres}}{3 \text{ hours}} \\\\
\dfrac{1500}{t}  &= \dfrac{45 \times 1000 \text{ metres}}{3 \times 60 \text{ minutes}} \\\\
\dfrac{1500}{t}  &= \dfrac{45 \times 1000}{3 \times 60}\\\\
\dfrac{1500}{t}  &= \dfrac{45000}{180}  \\\\
t                &= \dfrac{180 \times 1500}{45000}  \\\\
t                &= \dfrac{3 \times 6 \times 10 \times 15 \times 100}{3 \times 15 \times 10 \times 100}  \\\\
t                &= \dfrac{\cancel{3} \times 6 \times \cancel{10} \times \cancel{15} \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{3} \times \cancel{15} \times \cancel{10} \times \cancel{100}} \\\\
t                &= 6 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}  &= \dfrac{45}{3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 3}{3} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
              &= 15 \text{ kilometres/hour} \\\\
              &= 15 \times \dfrac{1000}{60} \text{ metres/minute} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 1000}{60} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{15 \times 250 \times 4}{15 \times 4} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{\cancel{15} \times 250 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{15} \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
              &= 250 \text{ metres/minute}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}  &=  \dfrac{1500}{250} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{150\cancel{0}}{25\cancel{0}} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{150}{25} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{5 \times 5 \times 6}{5 \times 5} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{\cancel{5} \times \cancel{5} \times 6}{\cancel{5} \times \cancel{5}} \\\\
                   &=  6 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Alternative solution
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
Let the time taken in minutes $= t$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{1500}{t}  &= \dfrac{45 \text{ kilometres}}{3 \text{ hours}} \\\\
\dfrac{1500}{t}  &= \dfrac{45 \times 1000 \text{ metres}}{3 \times 60 \text{ minutes}} \\\\
\dfrac{1500}{t}  &= \dfrac{45 \times 1000}{3 \times 60}\\\\
\dfrac{1500}{t}  &= \dfrac{45000}{180}  \\\\
t                &= \dfrac{180 \times 1500}{45000}  \\\\
t                &= \dfrac{3 \times 6 \times 10 \times 15 \times 100}{3 \times 15 \times 10 \times 100}  \\\\
t                &= \dfrac{\cancel{3} \times 6 \times \cancel{10} \times \cancel{15} \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{3} \times \cancel{15} \times \cancel{10} \times \cancel{100}} \\\\
t                &= 6 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6 \text{ minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A train travels $80\ \text{km}$ in $24 \ \text{minutes}$. How long will it take to travel $150\ \text{km}?$ 

- $48\ \text{minutes}$

- $\dfrac{1}{2}\ \text{hour}$

- $42\ \text{minutes}$

- $1\ \text{hour}$

- $45\ \text{minutes}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}       &= \dfrac{80}{24} \text{ km/minute} \\\\
\text{Time taken}  &= \dfrac{150}{\dfrac{80}{24}} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{150 \times 24}{80} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{15 \times 10 \times 3 \times 8}{8 \times 10} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel{10} \times 3 \times \cancel{8}}{\cancel{8} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
                   &=  15 \times 3 \\\\
                   &=  45 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>E</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$45 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}       &= \dfrac{80}{24} \text{ km/minute} \\\\
\text{Time taken}  &= \dfrac{150}{\dfrac{80}{24}} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{150 \times 24}{80} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{15 \times 10 \times 3 \times 8}{8 \times 10} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel{10} \times 3 \times \cancel{8}}{\cancel{8} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
                   &=  15 \times 3 \\\\
                   &=  45 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>E</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$45 \text{ minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Mr Sahota is cycling around a track at a constant speed. He completes $5$ laps every $4\ \text{minutes}$. How long will it take him to complete $8$ laps? Give your answer in minutes and seconds.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}                            &=  \dfrac{5 \text{ laps}}{4 \text{ minutes}}  \\\\
                                        &=  \dfrac{5}{4} \text{ laps/minute}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken to complete 8 laps}    &=  \dfrac{8}{\dfrac{5}{4}} \\\\
                                        &=  8 \times \dfrac{4}{5} \\\\
                                        &=  \dfrac{8 \times 4}{5} \\\\
                                        &=  \dfrac{32}{5} \\\\
                                        &=  6\dfrac{2}{5} \text{ minutes} \\\\
                                        &=  6 \text{ minutes } 24 \text { seconds}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \text{ minutes } 24 \text { seconds}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}                            &=  \dfrac{5 \text{ laps}}{4 \text{ minutes}}  \\\\
                                        &=  \dfrac{5}{4} \text{ laps/minute}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken to complete 8 laps}    &=  \dfrac{8}{\dfrac{5}{4}} \\\\
                                        &=  8 \times \dfrac{4}{5} \\\\
                                        &=  \dfrac{8 \times 4}{5} \\\\
                                        &=  \dfrac{32}{5} \\\\
                                        &=  6\dfrac{2}{5} \text{ minutes} \\\\
                                        &=  6 \text{ minutes } 24 \text { seconds}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

It takes Margaret $45\ \text{minutes}$ to cycle $9\ \text{km}$.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long would it take her to cycle $1\ \text{km}$ at that speed?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}      &=  \dfrac{9}{45} \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{9}{9 \times 5} \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{\cancel{9}}{\cancel{9} \times 5} \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{1}{5} \text{ km/minute} \\\\
\text{Time taken} &=  \dfrac{1}{\dfrac{1}{5}} \\\\
                  &=  5 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}      &=  \dfrac{9}{45} \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{9}{9 \times 5} \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{\cancel{9}}{\cancel{9} \times 5} \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{1}{5} \text{ km/minute} \\\\
\text{Time taken} &=  \dfrac{1}{\dfrac{1}{5}} \\\\
                  &=  5 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5 \text{ minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How far will she have travelled in one hour at that speed?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance travelled}  &= \dfrac{1}{5} \times 1 \text{ hour} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac{1}{5} \times (1 \times 60) \text{ minutes} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac{1}{5} \times 60 \\\\
                           &= \dfrac{60}{5} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac{12 \times 5}{5} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac{12 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
                           &= 12 \text{ km}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

$

</div>
<div class='workings'>

\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Total distance}} {\text{Total time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average speed}   &=  \dfrac{100}{2\dfrac{1}{2}} \\\\
                       &=  \dfrac{100}{\dfrac{5}{2}} \\\\
                       &=  \dfrac{100 \times 2}{5} \\\\
                       &=  \dfrac{20 \times 5 \times 2}{5} \\\\
                       &=  \dfrac{20 \times \cancel{5} \times 2}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
                       &=  20 \times 2 \\\\
                       &=  40 \text{ km/h} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

<p>8A0A521C-1CD1-48C0-A137-E2C9ADE58CEB</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-5:1:17</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prpcc</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Matthew reads at an average rate of $30 \ \text{pages per hour}$, while Alex reads at an average rate of $40 \ \text{pages per hour}$. If Matthew starts reading a novel at $4{:}30 \ \text{PM}$, and Alex begins reading the same novel at $5{:}20 \ \text{PM}$, at what time will they be reading the same page?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time}$


Let the time taken (in minutes) by Matthew $= t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
40 \times (t - 50) \text{ minutes}                &=  30 \times t \text{ minutes} \\\\
40 \times \dfrac{(t - 50)}{60} \text{ hours}      &=  30 \times \dfrac{t}{60} \text{ hours} \\\\
\dfrac{40 \times (t - 50)}{60}                    &= \dfrac{30 \times t}{60} \\\\
\dfrac{40t - 2000}{60}                            &= \dfrac{30t}{60} \\\\
\dfrac{40t - 2000}{60} - \dfrac{30t}{60}          &= 0 \\\\
\dfrac{40t - 2000 - 30t}{60}                      &= 0 \\\\
\dfrac{10t - 2000}{60}                            &= 0 \\\\
10t - 2000                                        &= 60 \times 0 \\\\
10t - 2000                                        &= 0 \\\\
10t                                               &= 2000 \\\\
t                                                 &= \dfrac{2000}{10} \\\\
t                                                 &= \dfrac{200 \times 10}{10} \\\\
t                                                 &= \dfrac{200 \times \cancel{10}}{\cancel{10}} \\\\
t                                                 &= 200 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}   &=   4{:}30 + 200 \text{ minutes} \\\\
              &=   7{:}50
\end{aligned}
$

At $7{:}50$, they will be reading the same page.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7{:}50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance
<emptyline>
Let the time taken (in minutes) by Matthew $= t$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
40 \times (t - 50) \text{ minutes}                &=  30 \times t \text{ minutes} \\\\
40 \times \dfrac{(t - 50)}{60} \text{ hours}      &=  30 \times \dfrac{t}{60} \text{ hours} \\\\
\dfrac{40 \times (t - 50)}{60}                    &= \dfrac{30 \times t}{60} \\\\
\dfrac{40t - 2000}{60}                            &= \dfrac{30t}{60} \\\\
\dfrac{40t - 2000}{60} - \dfrac{30t}{60}          &= 0 \\\\
\dfrac{40t - 2000 - 30t}{60}                      &= 0 \\\\
\dfrac{10t - 2000}{60}                            &= 0 \\\\
10t - 2000                                        &= 60 \times 0 \\\\
10t - 2000                                        &= 0 \\\\
10t                                               &= 2000 \\\\
t                                                 &= \dfrac{2000}{10} \\\\
t                                                 &= \dfrac{200 \times 10}{10} \\\\
t                                                 &= \dfrac{200 \times \cancel{10}}{\cancel{10}} \\\\
t                                                 &= 200 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}   &=   4{:}30 + 200 \text{ minutes} \\\\
              &=   7{:}50
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
At $7{:}50$, they will be reading the same page.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7{:}50$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

ABBR: $\text {Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance}   &=  12 \times (11 \text{ hours } 15  \ \text{minutes}) \\\\
                  &=  12 \times 11\dfrac{15}{60} \\\\
                  &=  12 \times 11\dfrac{15}{60} \\\\
                  &=  12 \times \dfrac{11 \times 60 + 15}{60}  \\\\
                  &=  12 \times \dfrac{660 + 15}{60}  \\\\
                  &=  12 \times \dfrac{675}{60}  \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{12 \times 675}{60}  \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{12 \times 135 \times 5}{12 \times 5}  \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{\cancel{12} \times 135 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{12} \times \cancel{5}}  \\\\
                  &=  135 \text{ m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$135 \text{ m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance}   &=  12 \times (11 \text{ hours } 15  \ \text{minutes}) \\\\
                  &=  12 \times 11\dfrac{15}{60} \\\\
                  &=  12 \times 11\dfrac{15}{60} \\\\
                  &=  12 \times \dfrac{11 \times 60 + 15}{60}  \\\\
                  &=  12 \times \dfrac{660 + 15}{60}  \\\\
                  &=  12 \times \dfrac{675}{60}  \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{12 \times 675}{60}  \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{12 \times 135 \times 5}{12 \times 5}  \\\\
                  &=  \dfrac{\cancel{12} \times 135 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{12} \times \cancel{5}}  \\\\
                  &=  135 \text{ m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

                    &= 3 \text{ hours} \\\\ 
\text{Distance}     &= 60 \times 3 \\\\
                    &= 180 \text{ m}

$

<div class='question subquestion'>

He catches a train back from Leicester to Birmingham, and travels at $66 \ \text{miles per hour}$. How long does the return journey take?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}  &=  \dfrac{44}{66} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{2 \times 2 \times 11}{2 \times 3 \times 11} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{2 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{11}}{\cancel{2} \times 3 \times \cancel{11}} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{2}{3} \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{2}{3} \text{ hours}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}  &=  \dfrac{44}{66} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{2 \times 2 \times 11}{2 \times 3 \times 11} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{2 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{11}}{\cancel{2} \times 3 \times \cancel{11}} \\\\
                   &=  \dfrac{2}{3} \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{2}{3} \text{ hours}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>

tspeed
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

It takes Katie $3 \ \text{hours}$ to jog $18  \ \text{miles}$ at a steady pace. If Katie decides to run for only two hours, but at the same speed how far would she run? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}   &=  \dfrac{18}{3} \\\\
               &=  \dfrac{6 \times 3}{3} \\\\
               &=  \dfrac{6 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
               &=  6 \text{ miles/hour} \\\\
\text{Distance}&=  6 \times 2 \\\\
               &=  12 \text{ miles}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \text{ miles}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Speed}   &=  \dfrac{18}{3} \\\\
               &=  \dfrac{6 \times 3}{3} \\\\
               &=  \dfrac{6 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
               &=  6 \text{ miles/hour} \\\\
\text{Distance}&=  6 \times 2 \\\\
               &=  12 \text{ miles}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12 \text{ miles}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>st-albans-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-2:1:25</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
